&lt;project_context>
# 项目: schema_semantic_constrained_ie

## 运行环境
- 服务器名: westd-16980
- SSH 账户: root（调用 ssh_execute / submit_training_job 时传入 account="root"）
- SSH 连接命令: ssh connect.westd.seetacloud.com  # 必须用此命令，~/.ssh/config 自动应用密钥和跳板机，禁止用裸 IP
- 项目代码根目录: /root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie


## 环境搭建
连接服务器后先调 `detect_image` 检测是否为预置镜像。如果匹配，跳过基础环境搭建，只装项目特有依赖。已预装的包不要重复安装。

## 输出风格
回复简洁直接。只输出执行结果和必要信息，不要解释过程、不要客套、不要给未被要求的建议。
&lt;/project_context>
## 任务：调查 S2 pilot 实验 relation_f1=0 的根因

### 背景
phase2_s2_pilot50 实验（S2 两通道约束解码，50 样本 pilot）结果：
- domain_range_violation_rate=0% ✅（PositionDetector 修复生效）
- relation_vocabulary_violation_rate=0% ✅
- relation_f1=0.0 ⚠️（25 predicted relations, 0 true positives, 21 gold relations）

violation 修复成功但 F1=0，说明模型能生成合法 relation，但内容全部错误。需要找出根因。

### 服务器
- 服务器：westd-16980，账号 root
- 项目路径：/root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie
- 环境：`source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base`

### 调查步骤

1. **找到 S2 pilot 输出文件**：
   ```bash
   ls -la /root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/outputs/*s2* /root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/outputs/*pilot*
   ```

2. **查看 predicted relations 的内容**：取 5-10 条 predicted relation，对比 gold label，看：
   - relation type 是否正确（在 7 个合法 type 中）？
   - head/tail entity 是否匹配 gold？
   - 是否有 span 边界偏移导致 exact match 失败？

3. **检查 S2 的第二通道（relation pass）prompt**：
   - 读 `run_constrained_extraction.py` 中 S2（two-pass）的 relation 生成逻辑
   - 确认第二通道 prompt 是否正确传入了第一通道的 entity 结果
   - 确认 relation 输出格式与评估脚本的期望格式一致

4. **对比 unconstrained baseline 的 relation 预测**：
   - Phase 0 unconstrained relation_f1=0.08，至少有一些 TP
   - S2 的 prompt 与 unconstrained 有何不同导致 F1 从 0.08 → 0？

5. **检查评估脚本**：
   - F1 计算是否用的 exact match（entity span + relation type 三元组必须完全匹配）？
   - 是否有 normalization 差异（大小写、空格、span 边界）？

### 产出
用简洁格式汇报：
1. 根因诊断（一句话）
2. 证据（3-5 条具体的 predicted vs gold 对比）
3. 修复建议（代码层面需要改什么）

  "command": "cd /root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie && source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base && python3 -c \"\nimport json\n\nwith open('outputs/phase2_s2_pilot50.jsonl') as f:\n    lines = [json.loads(l) for l in f]\n\n# Show stats\ntotal_pred = 0\ntotal_gold = 0\ntp = 0\n\nfor item in lines:\n    gold_rels = item.get('gold_relations', [])\n    pred = item.get('parsed_output', {})\n    pred_rels = pred.get('relations', [])\n    \n    total_pred += len(pred_rels)\n    total_gold += len(gold_rels)\n    \n    # Convert to tuples for matching\n    gold_set = set()\n    for r in gold_rels:\n        gold_set.add((r['head'], r['tail'], r['type']))\n    \n    for r in pred_rels:\n        key = (r['head'], r['tail'], r['type'])\n        if key in gold_set:\n            tp += 1\n\nprint(f'Total samples: {len(lines)}')\nprint(f'Total predicted relations: {total_pred}')\nprint(f'Total gold relations: {total_gold}')\nprint(f'True positives (head+tail+type match): {tp}')\nprint()\n\n# Show first 8 samples with relations\ncount = 0\nfor item in lines:\n    gold_rels = item.get('gold_relations', [])\n    pred = item.get('parsed_output', {})\n    pred_rels = pred.get('relations', [])\n    if not gold_rels and not pred_rels:\n        continue\n    \n    print(f'=== {item[\\\"doc_id\\\"]} ===')\n    print(f'Gold ({len(gold_rels)}):')\n    for r in gold_rels:\n        print(f'  ({r[\\\"head\\\"]}, {r[\\\"type\\\"]}, {r[\\\"tail\\\"]}) [{r[\\\"head_type\\\"]}->{r[\\\"tail_type\\\"]}]')\n    print(f'Pred ({len(pred_rels)}):')\n    for r in pred_rels:\n        print(f'  ({r[\\\"head\\\"]}, {r[\\\"type\\\"]}, {r[\\\"tail\\\"]}) [{r[\\\"head_type\\\"]}->{r[\\\"tail_type\\\"]}]')\n    print()\n    count += 1\n    if count >= 8:\n        break\n\"\n",

Extract entities and relations from the given text.\\n\\nEntity types: Task, Method, Material, Metric, OtherScientificTerm, Generic\\nRelation types: Used-for, Feature-of, Hyponym-of, Part-of, Compare, Conjunction, Evaluate-for\\n\\nOutput a JSON object with:\\n- \\\"entities\\\": list of {\\\"text\\\": \\\"...\\\", \\\"type\\\": \\\"...\\\"}\\n- \\\"relations\\\": list of {\\\"head\\\": \\\"...\\\", \\\"tail\\\": \\\"...\\\", \\\"type\\\": \\\"...\\\", \\\"head_type\\\": \\\"...\\\", \\\"tail_type\\\": \\\"...\\\"}\\n\\nOutput ONLY valid JSON, no explanation.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nSCIERC_USER_TEMPLATE = \\\"\\\"\\\"Extract all entities and relations from this scientific text:\\n\\n\\\"{text}\\\"\\n\\nOutput JSON:\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n\\n# -- Data Loading --\\n\\ndef load_scierc_data(data_path):\\n    samples = []\\n    with open(data_path) as f:\\n        for line in f:\\n            line = line.strip()\\n            if not line:\\n                continue\\n            doc = json.loads(line)\\n            doc_id = doc.get(\\\"doc_key\\\", \\\"unknown\\\")\\n            sentences = doc.get(\\\"sentences\\\", [])\\n            ner_all = doc.get(\\\"ner\\\", [])\\n            rel_all = doc.get(\\\"relations\\\", [])\\n\\n            offsets = []\\n            cum = 0\\n            for s in sentences:\\n                offsets.append(cum)\\n                cum += len(s)\\n\\n            for sent_idx, tokens in enumerate(sentences):\\n                sentence_text = \\\" \\\".join(tokens)\\n                offset = offsets[sent_idx]\\n\\n                gold_entities = []\\n                if sent_idx < len(ner_all):\\n                    for start, end, etype in ner_all[sent_idx]:\\n                        mention = \\\" \\\".join(tokens[start - offset:h_e - offset + 1])\\n                        tail_text = \\\" \\\".join(tokens[t_s - offset:t_e - offset + 1])\\n                        head_type = span_type.get((h_s, h_e), \\\"UNKNOWN\\\")\\n                        tail_type = span_type.get((t_s, t_e), \\\"UNKNOWN\\\")\\n                        rtype = RAW_TO_CANONICAL.get(rtype_raw, rtype_raw)\\n                        gold_relations.append({\\n                            \\\"head\\\": head_text, \\\"tail\\\": tail_text, \\\"type\\\": rtype,\\n                            \\\"head_type\\\": head_type, \\\"tail_type\\\": tail_type,\\n                        })\\n\\n                samples.append({\\n                    \\\"doc_id\\\": f\\\"{doc_id}_sent{sent_idx}\\\",\\n                    \\\"input_text\\\": sentence_text,\\n          \\n\\n... raw_p1, {\\\"entities\\\": [], \\\"relations\\\": []}\\n\\n    # -- Pass 2: Relation extraction with entity constraints --\\n    entity_texts = list({e[\\\"text\\\"] for e in entities if \\\"text\\\"        out_p2 = model.generate(\\n            **inp_p2, max_new_tokens=max_new_tokens,\\n            do_sample=False, logits_processor=procs_p2,\\n        )\\n    raw_p2 = <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can see the patterns clearly. Let me analyze the dryrun's 0 TP:

1. **Entity span mismatch**: Gold uses full spans with special tokens (e.g., "Multi-lingual Evaluation Task -LRB- MET -RRB-") but model predicts without them (e.g., "Multi-lingual Evaluation Task"), or splits them (e.g., "Named Entity -LRB- NE -RRB- items" vs "Named Entity")

2. **Wrong relation type**: Model picks wrong relation type even when entities are close (e.g., "morphological analysis, Part-of, japanese text processing" vs gold "morphological analysis, Used-for, japanese text processing")


3. **Incorrect entity selection**: The model sometimes grabs the wrong entity text for head or tail positions, and occasionally swaps them entirely—pulling completely different entities than what the gold annotation specifies. There are also nonsensical self-referential relations where the same entity appears as both head and tail, and issues with how abbreviations are handled versus their full forms.

Now I'm looking at the S2 relation prompt to understand if there's a structural problem with how it's set up.

_source line 96 · 2026-05-13T23:43:56.924Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now let me do a systematic error analysis. Let me categorize the errors in the dryrun:

From the dryrun data (10 samples, 25 predicted, 21 gold, 0 TP):

**Error categories:**

1. **Entity span mismatch** (head/tail text differs from gold):
   - Most predictions use entities that don't match gold entity spans
   - e.g., Gold "proper nouns" → not in pred; Gold "Named Entity -LRB- NE -RRB- items" → pred uses "Named Entity" (partial)
   - Gold "recognition and classification of proper names , numerical and temporal expressions" → pred splits into "proper names" and "numerical and temporal expressions"

2. **Wrong relation type**:
   - "morphological analysis, Part-of, japanese text processing" vs gold "morphological analysis, Used-for, japanese text processing" 

3. **Wrong entity pairing** (entities exist but wrong head-tail combination):
   - Gold: (Amorph, Used-for, NE items) → Pred: (Amorph, Used-for, dictionary lookup)
   - Gold: (dictionaries, Used-for, it) → Pred: (segment, Used-for, dictionaries)

4. **Self-referential nonsense**:
   - (Amorph, Used-for, Amorph) and similar cases where the model predicts an entity paired with itself

5. **Abbreviation splitting**:
   - Gold: "Multi-lingual Evaluation Task -LRB- MET -RRB-" as one entity → Pred: splits into "Multi-lingual Evaluation Task" and "MET", then creates "Conjunction" between them
   - Gold: "Named Entity -LRB- NE -RRB- items" → Pred: "Named Entity" and "NE", with Hyponym-of between them

The core issue is that entity span mismatches are cascading into relation errors. With entity F1 only around 0.27-0.31, most predicted entities have incorrect text spans, and since relations require both head and tail to match exactly, this creates a domino effect of relation failures.

Looking at the math: a relation is only counted as correct if the head, tail, and type all match. Given that entity F1 is ~0.27, the probability of both entities in a relation being correct is roughly 0.27² ≈ 0.07, which explains the extremely low relation F1. Interestingly, the pilot50 results show 13 true positives with F1=0.10, which is actually comparable to the unconstrained baseline at F1=0.08, so the S2 constraint approach isn't making performance worse—it's just about equivalent.

The real bottleneck is the base model's entity extraction quality itself, not the S2 architecture. That said, there are some S2-specific issues emerging, including self-referential relations.

I'm noticing the model generates relations where the head and tail are identical, which should never happen—the grammar needs to prevent this. There's also a systematic pattern where abbreviations like "X -LRB- Y -RRB-" get split into separate entities with incorrect Conjunction or Hyponym relations created between them.

Looking at the prompt design, the S2 Pass2 prompt claims "head and tail must be entity texts from the given list," but the grammar allows any string as a fallback, and the model still generates entities outside that list. I need to check whether the entity_texts enum is actually being enforced correctly in the grammar.

The code tries to build a relation schema with entity_texts constrained to the Pass 1 entities, falling back to free-text head/tail if that compilation fails. So relations can only reference entities from Pass 1, which are already incorrect compared to gold—that's expected. But self-referential relations should still be blocked by the grammar itself. I need to examine the build_relation_only_schema function to see if it's allowing head==tail, and then I'll have enough to write up the findings.

Now let me also check how the unconstrained baseline performs on the first 10 samples to see if it does any better.
```

{
  "server": "westd-16980",
  "command": "cd /root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie && source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base && python3 -c \"\nimport json\n\n# Get dryrun doc_ids\nwith open('outputs/phase2_s2_dryrun.jsonl') as f:\n    dryrun = [json.loads(l) for l in f]\ndryrun_ids = {d['doc_id'] for d in dryrun}\n\n# Find same samples in unconstrained\nwith open('outputs/phase2_scierc_unconstrained.jsonl') as f:\n    unconstrained = [json.loads(l) for l in f]\n\ndef rel_key(r):\n    return (r.get('head','').lower().strip(), r.get('tail','').lower().strip(), r.get('type',''))\n\n# Compare\nfor doc_id in sorted(dryrun_ids):\n    s2 = [d for d in dryrun if d['doc_id'] == doc_id][0]\n    uc_list = [d for d in unconstrained if d['doc_id'] == doc_id]\n    if not uc_list:\n        continue\n    uc = uc_list[0]\n    \n    gold_rels = s2.get('gold_relations', [])\n    if not gold_rels:\n        continue\n    \n    gold_keys = set(rel_key(r) for r in gold_rels)\n    \n    s2_parsed = s2.get('parsed_output', {})\n    s2_rels = s2_parsed.get('relations', [])\n    s2_keys = set(rel_key(r) for r in s2_rels)\n    s2_tp = len(gold_keys & s2_keys)\n    \n    uc_parsed = uc.get('parsed_output', {})\n    uc_rels = uc_parsed.get('relations', []) if uc_parsed else []\n    uc_keys = set(rel_key(r) for r in uc_rels)\n    uc_tp = len(gold_keys & uc_keys)\n    \n    print(f'{doc_id}: gold={len(gold_rels)}, S2_pred={len(s2_rels)}/tp={s2_tp}, UC_pred={len(uc_rels)}/tp={uc_tp}')\n    \n    # Show UC predictions\n    if uc_tp > s2_tp:\n        uc_matches = gold_keys & uc_keys\n        print(f'  UC matches: {uc_matches}')\n        print(f'  S2 pred: {[rel_key(r) for r in s2_rels]}')\n\"\n",
  "account": "root"
}

Now I have a clear picture. Let me summarize:

**Entity-level analysis:**
- Entity F1 (text+type): 57 TP / 197 pred / 175 gold → P=0.29, R=0.33, F1=0.31
- Text-only match: 99 out of 197 predictions match gold text (50%)
- This means ~42 entities have correct text but wrong type (99-57=42)

**Relation-level analysis:**
- 146 predicted relations, 112 gold, 13 TP → F1=0.10


- Looking at the false positives, the core issue is clear: over half (70 out of 133) have both entities completely wrong, which points back to poor entity extraction as the root cause. Another 30 have the head entity right but tail wrong, 16 have the opposite, and a small fraction have matching entities but wrong relation types or swapped arguments. Only 17 of the 146 predicted relations even have both entities matching the gold standard text.

The real problem isn't the relation classification or constraint decoding—it's that the base model fundamentally struggles to extract entity spans that align with the gold annotations. The S2 architecture is doing its job constraining relation types and domain/range correctly, but it's working with poor entity boundaries to begin with. The dryrun's F1=0 makes sense now too: those first 10 samples come from just two documents with particularly difficult entity spans that the model can't handle. E -RRB- items", "Multi-lingual Evaluation Task -LRB- MET -RRB-"). The unconstrained baseline also scores 0 on 9/10 of these samples.

**Specific S2 issues:**
1. Self-referential relations (6 cases) - grammar should block head==tail
2. Pass1 entity types disagree with Pass2 head_type/tail_type (cross-field consistency violations = 14)

**Summary for the user:**

1. **Root cause**: The base model (likely Qwen-7B class) has poor entity span extraction quality (entity F1=0.31). Since relation evaluation requires BOTH head AND tail text to match exactly, even moderate entity errors cause relation F1 to collapse. In 52.6% of false positives, both head and tail text are wrong.

2. **The f1=0 in dryrun is a sampling artifact** — the first 10 samples come from 2 documents with particularly challenging entity spans like parenthetical abbreviations, while pilot50 shows f1=0.10, which is comparable to the unconstrained baseline at f1=0.08.

3. **S2 architecture isn't the bottleneck** — constrained decoding successfully prevents constraint violations but can't compensate for the underlying entity extraction quality issues in the base model.

4. **What needs to happen**: Improve entity extraction through better few-shot examples or a fine-tuned model, add a grammar rule to prevent self-referential relations, and normalize head/tail text during evaluation to handle tokenization artifacts like -LRB-/-RRB-.

So the core issue is entity quality, not the S2 framework itself.

分析完成。以下是诊断结果：

---

## 根因诊断

**relation_f1≈0 的根因是 base model 的 entity span 提取质量太差（entity F1=0.31），不是 S2 约束解码架构的问题。** relation 评估要求 head+tail+type 三元组完全匹配，entity 错误以平方级放大到 relation。

注：用户引用的"25 pred, 0 TP, 21 gold"实际来自 `violation_report_s2_dryrun.json`（10 样本），不是 pilot50。pilot50 实际 relation_f1=0.1008（13 TP/146 pred/112 gold），与 unconstrained baseline 的 0.0823 基本持平。

## 证据

**1. FP 错误分类（133 个 FP，pilot50）：**

| 类别 | 数量 | 占比 |
|------|------|------|
| head+tail 都不匹配 gold | 70 | 52.6% |
| head 对但 tail 错 | 30 | 22.6% |
| tail 对但 head 错 | 16 | 12.0% |
| head+tail 互换 | 7 | 5.3% |
| head+tail 对但 type 错 | 4 | 3.0% |
| 自引用 (head==tail) | 6 | 4.5% |

**2. 具体对比（dryrun 样本）：**

```
X96-1059_sent3:
  Gold: (morphological analyzer, Used-for, recognition and classification of proper names , numerical and temporal expressions)
  Pred: (morphological analyzer, Used-for, proper names)   ← tail 是 gold 的子串
  Pred: (morphological analyzer, Used-for, numerical and temporal expressions)  ← 拆分了

X96-1059_sent2:
  Gold: (approach, Used-for, Multi-lingual Evaluation Task -LRB- MET -RRB-)
  Pred: (Multi-lingual Evaluation Task, Conjunction, MET)  ← 把缩写拆成两个 entity 造 Conjunction

X96-1059_sent5:
  Gold: (Amorph, Used-for, NE items)
  Pred: (Amorph, Used-for, dictionary lookup)  ← 正确 head 但选错 tail

X96-1059_sent8:
  Pred: (NE item, Feature-of, NE item)  ← 自引用
```

**3. Entity 层面的质量指标：**
- text+type 匹配：57/197 pred（P=0.29）
- text-only 匹配（忽略 type）：99/197（50%）
- 42 个 entity text 对了但 type 标错 → Pass2 的 head_type/tail_type 也跟着错

**4. S2 vs unconstrained 同样本对比（dryrun 10 样本）：**
- S2: 0 TP, unconstrained: 1 TP — 基本一样差，不是 S2 引入的退化

## 修复建议

1. **核心问题（entity 质量）**：在 Pass1 entity prompt 中加 2-3 个 few-shot 示例（从 SciERC train 抽），示范正确的 span 边界（特别是含 `-LRB-`/`-RRB-` 的长 span）。当前是 zero-shot，7B 模型没能力猜对 span 边界

2. **快速修复**：
   - `build_relation_only_schema` 中加约束阻止 head==tail 的自引用关系（6 个无意义 FP）
   - 评估时做 entity text normalization：strip `-LRB-`/`-RRB-` 括号，统一子串匹配，提升 recall 上限

3. **结构性改进**：Pass2 prompt 中把 entity list 改为编号引用格式（`[E1] morphological analyzer (Method)`），让模型通过引用 ID 选 entity 而非重新生成文本，避免 span 抄写错误
